# Board

Live tasks, a log and questions for the owner. The rules are in `docs/TEAM.md`, the plan in `docs/PILOT-PLAN.md`.

- **To claim a task:** set its owner and status in the first commit of your work branch.
- **Status values:** `todo`, `doing (branch)`, `review (PR #)`, `done`, `blocked (why)`.

## Inbox for Codex

Codex checks this section on a schedule; it is how Claude hands Codex work and replies (GitHub `@codex` mentions start
reviews but cannot start coding work in this repository's Codex setup). Newest first. Codex marks an item `taken`
when it starts and `done (PR #n)` when its pull request is open.

| 8 Oct | Claude | **Hook the 3D workday up to the hub and the results (A-3, branch `claude/workday-flow`, described in `docs/WORKDAY-EVENTS.md`, "Starting and finishing a workday").** Do it after C-6, in `FirstPersonScene.js` (and `fpmission.js` for the stop number), as its own `codex/workday-hooks` branch once A-3 is merged. (1) **Start data:** the hub starts `'FirstPersonScene'` with `{ workday: { resume } }`. When `data.workday` is there, skip the 3D hub and go straight to the campaign: with `resume: false` discard any saved campaign first (`progress.campaign = null`), with `resume: true` restore it (as `launch('campaign')` does). (2) **Leaving mid-day:** in a hub-started workday, "Save and return to hub" and "Main game" checkpoint, then call `OTR.workday.pause(this)` (it returns to the 2D hub; do not reload the page). (3) **Finishing:** when "Finish workday" succeeds (`m.finish()` returns nothing), clear `progress.campaign` as now, then call `OTR.workday.finish(this, { seconds: Math.round(m.elapsed), stops: m.stops.map(s => ({ stop: s.id + 1, delivered: s.outcome === 'handover' \|\| s.outcome === 'safeplace', address: s.address, x: s.x, z: s.z })), depot: { x, z } })` in place of `showDebrief` (it opens the results; the debrief there is Claude's). (4) **Stop numbers count from 1:** `report()` sends `stop: this.parcel(key).stop` and `Number(key)`, which are 0-based ids, so the debrief would say "Stop 0"; send `id + 1` as `stop` (keep the keys as they are). ### Milestone 4: corporate requirements

Milestone 3 is complete on `pilot`. Milestone 4 (`docs/PILOT-PLAN.md`) is the company theme, sign-in, offline and
firewall use, reporting to training systems, data handling, accessibility in 3D and the before-and-after trainer report.
Already in place from the 2D game and earlier tasks: neutral theme file (A-1), employee ID sign-in (A-5), SCORM 1.2/2004
packaging and suspend data, key remapping, larger text and a colour-blind filter on Phaser cameras. One task at a time
per agent, in order. Codex first.

### Milestones 7 to 10: the work package (owner brief, 17 October 2026)

The owner played it and said it is not close yet: the 3D mechanics are basic, the graphics look rough, driving feels
off (reverse most of all), and getting out to look every time is impractical. Decisions taken (all by the owner):

- **Physical cab.** The mirrors are real objects that are always there and always live; checking them means looking at
  them, not pressing a key. Changing gear means looking at a physical shifter (P, R, N, D) and pressing the action key.
  The same idea for handbrake, indicator, headlights, wipers and seatbelt (keyboard shortcuts stay as an option).
- **Rear camera.** It comes on by itself when the van goes into reverse (guidelines that bend with the steering, a
  distance colour band, sensor beeps). It replaces getting out to look; a walk-around stays as an optional, quick step.
- **Forgiving but weighty** driving: easy to place the van, but it has mass.
- **Asset files are allowed** (free CC0 models and textures, bundled locally, glTF; see A-20). Looks must improve a lot.
- **Street signs and a sat nav** (a dash-mounted map screen with the route and turn-by-turn). Couriers do not use paper.
- **Also in:** dashboard gauges, working wipers, night and dusk driving with headlights, load that shifts, a van that can
  be damaged, low clearances, traffic and pedestrians that behave, a cyclist in the blind spot, richer stops
  (apartments, intercoms, gated sites, reception, lockers, reattempts), road works and diversions, dispatch radio calls,
  and a replay of the last mistake.
- **Damage must be reported.** At the pre-trip walkaround the player records damage that is already there. At the post
  trip the player must report and record any damage they caused. An unreported damage they caused is a critical mistake.

**Order.** Tasks keep the order below inside each owner's list and stop at "after" notes; a later task never changes what
an earlier merged task already guarantees (scores, saves, accessibility, the Low setting). Claude's A-19 (scoring
contract) and A-20 (asset policy) go first. The contract is in `docs/WORKDAY-EVENTS.md`.

Rules that apply to every task here:
- Low graphics stays playable on the pilot laptops (`test/fpbench.js` gates; document figures in `docs/PERF.md`).
- Every new control keeps a keyboard/accessible route and works with larger text, colour-blind and reduced motion.
- New text is whole English sentences (Claude translates). New events use the types in `WORKDAY-EVENTS.md` only.
- The first workday stays gentle; the new aids start on and fade out on later days or when the trainer turns them off.
- Each milestone ends with screenshots and a short clip per task in `docs/review/set-<n>/` and a five-minute checklist for the
  owner, so a review does not need a full test (A-23).

## Questions for the owner

Questions marked `needs-owner` on GitHub are listed here too. Work on the rest carries on while they wait.

1. **The pilot company:**
   - Does it need results sent to its training system (an LMS such as Cornerstone, Workday or SAP SuccessFactors)?
   - Are there data rules we must meet?

   Ask again when the pilot company is confirmed. Until then, build for the general case.

2. **C-1/C-7, needs-owner:** does the expanded five-stop 3D workday still hold 60 fps on the Intel laptop with low graphics? Run the updated benchmark, including mirrors at all five stops.
   Cloud Chromium uses SwiftShader, so its benchmark cannot establish the laptop's frame rate.

3. **LMS completion (A-10):** a company's LMS now sees the course as complete only once a 3D workday is finished, and
   as passed once the best day met the pass marks (and every module is passed). Is that right for the pilot? To go back
   to modules only, set `lmsWorkday: false` in `data/config.js`.

4. **The trainer's quick start (B-43):** `docs/QUICK-START.md` is one printable page for whoever runs a session, in
   English. Which languages do the pilot's trainers need it in? Until you say, it stays English only (the game itself is
   in all twelve).

5. ~~**Codex has not pushed since about 07:00 UTC (20 Oct).**~~ Withdrawn 12:35 UTC: Codex is back (C-34 pushed at 12:30, with the locker answer and the sound-test fix taken).

6. **A workday resumed on another PC (B-70).** Where a trainee is in the 3D town (which stops are done, the van, the clock) is kept in that PC's browser only; the course progress goes to the server or the LMS. So a trainee who stops mid-day and comes back on a different PC starts that day again from the morning. The game now asks first, and Back keeps the day for the PC it was started on. On shared training PCs, should the place in town travel with the trainee? It can on the training server (the progress file has room), and with SCORM 2004 (64,000 characters). SCORM 1.2 has no room for it (4,096 characters, already nearly full). Measured for B-82 on days 1, 4 and 9: the 3D checkpoint is 13,500 to 15,600 characters at the start of a day and 18,100 to 20,400 mid-day (three stops done, 120 mirror checks). That is four to five times the whole of SCORM 1.2's room, fits SCORM 2004's 64,000 beside the course save with about 40,000 to spare, and is small for the training server's file. Default meanwhile: same-PC resume, as now; the quick start and the pilot guide say so.
7. **SCORM 2004 by default (B-101).** On the heaviest later days, SCORM 1.2 has 40 characters left of its 4,000 (B-98); past that, a day in progress loses its drive-map pins when resumed (the score is kept). SCORM 2004 has 64,000. Most LMSs take both. Shall the pilot package be SCORM 2004, with SCORM 1.2 still available for an LMS that needs it? Default meanwhile: 2004 is built by default, and 1.2 with a flag.

8. **2D scenes on PCs without a graphics driver (B-87).** On such a PC (a software renderer), the stop scenes run at 12 to 18 fps; module 7 runs at 30. The cost is the number of full-screen layers each frame draws (`docs/PERF.md`, B-87), not the code. Reaching 25 fps means drawing less on those PCs only: (a) leave out the colour grade and the heat overlays there (about +5 fps, flatter colours and no vignette), or (b) also draw at a lower resolution there (likely 25 fps or more, slightly softer). The game can tell when it is on a software renderer. **Default until you say: no change.** Every scene stays above the QA floor of 10 fps, and the same layers on a laptop with Intel graphics are not the problem. **You can try (a) now (B-126):** add `&gfx=lite` to the address. On a software renderer the heat scene goes from 14 to 22 fps and the others from about 19 to 22–23; pictures side by side are in [`review/set-9/b126/`](review/set-9/b126/README.md). If you want it, the next step is to switch it on automatically on such PCs.

9. ~~**Codex has not pushed since 17:45 UTC (21 Oct).**~~ Withdrawn 23:45 UTC: Codex is back (C-36 evidence, C-37 acceptance, and C-48 from your visual review).

10. **Driving at night without headlights: how serious?** Today it is a check worth 2 safety points per leg (`drive.lights`). On a day with nothing else wrong, a leg in the dark without lights leaves safety at 92%, still three stars (`workday-3d-l`). Driving through a red light is critical and caps safety at one star. Should no headlights at night be critical too, or cost a star (for example worth 6 points)? **Default until you say: as now (points only).** It is one line in `data/workday_events.js`.

### Answered

- **Test laptop (7 October):** the owner's own laptop (Intel integrated graphics, the one used for the 25 September
  check). The 60 fps floor is measured there. Ask for a measurement with `needs-owner`.
- **Logins (7 October):** usually an employee ID number and a password. This is task A-5. LMS and company sign-in
  stay supported.

- **21 October 2026, Claude — B-87 measured:** on SwiftShader the slow 2D scenes (12 to 18 fps) are limited by pixels filled, not by code. The CPU is over 90% idle, and each full-screen layer (sky, colour grade, an open card's dimmer, the heat overlays) costs about 9 ms a frame. They draw 4 to 6 screens a frame, module 7 about 2.5. No layer can be merged without drawing the same pixels, so reaching 25 fps means drawing less on those PCs, which changes the look: owner question 8, default no change. Figures in `docs/PERF.md`; new `test/tools/overdraw.js` lists what a scene draws.
